# RShopRefills V2 - Project Memory

> Last updated: 2026-09-17 (verified stack summary and current guide references; earlier session history retained)
> Purpose: Session resume file. On every new session, read this first to restore full context before making any changes.
> Current references: [README stack](README.md#tech-stack) and [working rules and design system](roddy-tech-website-rules.md). Older implementation and design notes below are dated history; verify them against current code before applying them.

---

## Project Vision

**RShopRefills V2** is a production-grade multi-vendor marketplace and payment platform.

Core goals:

- Multi-vendor marketplace (vendors list products, customers buy/refill)
- Manual verification + billing workflows
- Wallet system for vendors and customers
- Admin dashboard (SaaS-style, dashboard-heavy)
- Pusher real-time integrations + email notifications
- Vendor settlement / payout workflows

---

## Team & Workflow Rules

- Team project, multiple developers
- Branch workflow: feature branches into develop into main
- Never commit directly to main
- Never force push
- Never hardcode local DB credentials or expose production keys
- Each developer uses their own local database

### Role split

- **Roddy Tech (frontend engineer):** owns UI / design only. Blade, Tailwind, Flux/Volt markup, Alpine state, copy. Reads backend files to learn contracts, never modifies them.
- **Backend team:** owns controllers, models, migrations, routes, middleware, validation rules, mail config, `.env`.

### Frontend workflow

Before any UI task:

1. Check what the backend team has already implemented (Volt PHP blocks, controllers, routes, models, validation rules, events).
2. Then do exactly one of two things:
   - **Build frontend only** when no backend exists yet. Design the markup with sensible `wire:model` / form field names so backend can wire it up later.
   - **Wire frontend to existing backend** when backend exists. Bind to the existing properties, methods, validation messages, routes, and events. Match what is there exactly.

Never modify PHP logic, validation rules, route definitions, middleware, models, or `.env`. If something on the backend looks broken or missing, flag it as FYI for the backend team and stop.

| Mail | Resend selected locally | Service delivery was not verified |

| Auth | Laravel Socialite and application auth services | Socialite 5.27.0 |

Tailwind v4 is configured through `@tailwindcss/vite`, with `@theme` in `resources/css/app.css`. Local driver selections are a dated snapshot and do not establish production configuration.

## Design Conventions

Historical design notes from earlier sessions are retained below. For current work, follow [the verified working rules](roddy-tech-website-rules.md), the current stylesheet and component, and the user's latest instructions. The earlier font, surface, theme, and radius assumptions in this history are not current global requirements.

- **Brand color:** solid blue (`blue-600` primary, `blue-700` hover, `blue-950` dark panels). No purple, no violet.
- **No gradients.** Use solid colors only. Avoid `bg-gradient-to-*`, `from-*`, `via-*`, `to-*` and inline gradients.
- **No em or en dashes** in code, copy, comments, or commit messages. Use commas, periods, parentheses, or rewrite the sentence.
- **Copyright year:** hardcoded `2026`. Do not use `date('Y')`.
- **Action button arrows:** size `w-5 h-5` (20px), not the default `w-4 h-4`.
- **Storefront is always light mode.** `@fluxAppearance` is intentionally omitted from the storefront layout so Flux never injects a dark class.
- **Page surround color:** `#dbeafe` (Tailwind `blue-100`) — used as the body + content area background on both admin and customer dashboards. Replaces the older `#f1f6fb` (admin) and `#f5f7fb` (customer) tones.
- **Badges:** all badges across the site use `rounded-[5px]` (not `rounded-full`) with a **solid saturated background + white text** (e.g. `bg-blue-600 text-white`, `bg-emerald-500 text-white`, `bg-amber-500 text-white`, `bg-red-500 text-white`). Status pills follow the same rule (no -50/-100 tint + dark text combos).
- **Card style:** `rounded-2xl` (or `rounded-[20px]` on admin), `bg-white`, `shadow-sm shadow-zinc-900/5 ring-1 ring-zinc-100`. Icon tiles inside cards use `bg-blue-100` (light) or `bg-XXX-200` (admin KPI cards for stronger contrast against the blue-100 page bg).
- **Sidebar angle:** content area has `rounded-tl-[20px]` against a white outer to create a visible "tab" where the sidebar meets the content. Both admin and customer dashboards use this.

## Architecture Conventions

### Laravel 12 Structure

- No `Kernel.php`. Middleware registered in `bootstrap/app.php`.
- No `app/Console/Kernel.php`. Console routes in `routes/console.php`.
- Service providers in `bootstrap/providers.php`.

### Frontend / UI Pattern

- **Flux components** for the dashboard / admin area: `<flux:sidebar>`, `<flux:navlist>`, `<flux:button>`, `<flux:menu>`.
- **Volt single-file components** for interactive pages (PHP logic + Blade in one `.blade.php`).
- **Blade anonymous components** for static layout pieces and shared partials.
- **Alpine.js** for client-side state on the storefront (modals, dropdowns, transitions).
- Sidebar layout as the primary dashboard shell.

### Routing

- Named routes everywhere. Use `route('name')`, not hardcoded URLs.
- Volt routes via `Volt::route('path', 'component-name')`.
- Auth routes isolated in `routes/auth.php`.
- Web routes in `routes/web.php`.

### Key File Locations

| Purpose                         | Path                                                                  |
| ------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Storefront layout (header shell) | `resources/views/components/layouts/app/header.blade.php`            |
| Storefront nav partials         | `resources/views/components/nav/*.blade.php`                          |
| Dashboard sidebar layout        | `resources/views/components/layouts/app/sidebar.blade.php`            |
| App layout wrapper              | `resources/views/components/layouts/app.blade.php`                    |
| Auth layout (split panel)       | `resources/views/components/layouts/auth.blade.php`                   |
| Auth Volt pages                 | `resources/views/livewire/auth/*.blade.php`                           |
| Volt components                 | `resources/views/livewire/`                                           |
| Blade components                | `resources/views/components/`                                         |
| Custom Flux overrides           | `resources/views/flux/`                                               |
| Web routes                      | `routes/web.php`                                                      |
| Auth routes (incl. Google OAuth) | `routes/auth.php`                                                    |
| Google OAuth controller         | `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/GoogleAuthController.php`                  |
| Models                          | `app/Models/`                                                         |
| Service providers               | `app/Providers/`                                                      |
| Migrations                      | `database/migrations/`                                                |
| Static assets (logo, graphics)  | `public/assets/`                                                      |

### Auth system separated by guard (done, uncommitted)

Backend shipped a multi-auth architecture in `4c42c50` + `f0e88cd`: a separate `Admin` model + `admin` guard + `AdminAuthService` + `AdminAuth` middleware + dedicated `routes/admin.php`. Our frontend was still routing `/admin/*` content pages under the `web` guard, which would let any logged-in customer hit admin URLs. This session aligned the frontend with the backend's separation.

**Two completely separate auth flows, isolated sessions:**

| | Customer (`web` guard) | Admin (`admin` guard) |
|---|---|---|
| Model | `User` | `Admin` (separate table, role enum) |
| Login URL | `/login` (Volt) | `/admin/login` (Blade form) |
| Auth handler | Volt component, `Auth::attempt()` | `AdminAuthService` (rate limit, session regen, deactivation check, audit log) |
| Middleware | `auth + verified` | `admin` (re-checks `is_active` per request) |
| Routes file | `routes/web.php` + `routes/auth.php` | `routes/admin.php` |
| Dashboard | `/dashboard` (welcome stub) | `/admin/dashboard` (KPIs) |
| Content pages | none (customer area is the storefront) | `/admin/{products,orders,customers,transactions,wallets}` |
| Layout | `<x-layouts.app.header>` | `<x-layouts.admin>` |
| Logout | `route('logout')` | `route('admin.logout')` |

**Concrete changes this session:**

- `routes/web.php`: removed the misrouted `/admin/*` block (orders, customers, transactions, wallets were under `auth + verified`, wrong guard — would have leaked the admin layout to any verified customer).
- `routes/admin.php`: added 5 view routes (`admin.products`, `admin.orders`, `admin.customers`, `admin.transactions`, `admin.wallets`) inside the existing `admin` middleware group, alongside the shipped `admin.dashboard`.
- `components/layouts/admin.blade.php`: wired the 5 sidebar nav links + the "All Products" subitem to their real `route('admin.*')` names. Active-state highlighting via `request()->routeIs('admin.orders*')` etc. works end-to-end. The 6 product-category subitems (gift-cards, eSIMs, mobile-topups, bill-payments, flights, stays) and 4 bottom items (reports, marketing, support-tickets, settings) stay as `href="#"` until those views ship.
- Deleted 3 unused Laravel starter-kit auth layouts (`components/layouts/auth/{split,card,simple}.blade.php`). Only `centered.blade.php` is actively used (by forgot/reset/verify/confirm pages).

**Customer auth flows (login/register/forgot/reset/verify/confirm) were already aligned** with backend conventions — stock Laravel patterns matching the `User` model. Google OAuth controller now delegates to backend's `GoogleAuthService` (3 scenarios: returning Google user → existing email + link → brand-new user; fires `Registered` event for wallet auto-creation).

**Backend gaps flagged (FYI for backend team):**

- Duplicate `avatar` column on `users` table. Backend's canonical is `avatar_url` (used by `GoogleAuthService` everywhere). The frontend's old migration `2026_05_13_073004_add_google_columns_to_users_table.php` later added a second `avatar` column; no view reads it. Dead column + obsolete migration ready to drop.
- The 10 pending admin sidebar items above need real routes/views when backend ships them.

## Dev Environment Notes

- Run dev server: `composer run dev` (PHP server + queue + Vite together).
- Or individually: `php artisan serve` + `npm run dev`.
- **Vite port on Windows:** if `npm run dev` fails with `listen EACCES: permission denied ::1:5173`, the port is inside a Hyper-V/WinNAT reserved range (`netsh interface ipv4 show excludedportrange protocol=tcp`; 5169-5268 was reserved on 2026-09-12). Vite only auto-increments on EADDRINUSE, not EACCES, so pick a port outside every range: `npm run dev -- --port 5300`. Ranges can shift after a reboot.
- **`composer install` on Windows needs two skip flags:** `composer install --ignore-platform-req=ext-pcntl --ignore-platform-req=ext-posix`. `laravel/horizon` declares both extensions and neither exists on Windows PHP, so a plain `composer install` aborts with "Your lock file does not contain a compatible set of packages". This is a platform fact, not lockfile drift: do NOT run `composer update` to "fix" it. Horizon itself only needs those extensions to run its worker process, which happens on the server, so ignoring them locally costs nothing.
- DB: MySQL. Set your own credentials in `.env`, not committed.
- Default `.env` DB: `DB_DATABASE=app`, `DB_USERNAME=root`, `DB_PASSWORD=`.
- If frontend changes do not appear: `npm run build` or `npm run dev`.
- **Be consistent with `localhost` vs `127.0.0.1`** when developing locally. Switching between them mid-session breaks OAuth state validation and any other session-scoped flow.
- **Windows PHP CA bundle** (one-time setup): download `cacert.pem` from <https://curl.se/ca/cacert.pem>, save to a stable location, then add `curl.cainfo` and `openssl.cafile` to `php.ini` pointing at that file. The Google sign-in controller has a local-only workaround that disables SSL verify, but installing the CA bundle is the proper fix.

| 2026-05-17 | Roddy Tech | Checkout `price_updated` bug fix + scrollbar policy. **Bug:** every checkout failed with "Cart contains invalid or unavailable items: price_updated". Root cause in `CartValidationService::validateItem` — it compared the cart item's snapshotted unit price against a freshly recomputed one with a strict `!==` on floats. Both values come from the SAME `CartPricingService::calculatePricing`, so they are economically identical, but a money value like 11.01 is not exactly representable in binary and the snapshot also round-trips through a `decimal:4` DB cast, so `!==` reports a false difference and blocks checkout for every fixed-denomination product. **Fix:** compare with a 1-cent tolerance (`abs(a - b) > 0.01`). Verified catalog-wide by tracing the code: `unit_price_snapshot` is written by `CartPricingService::calculatePricing` and re-checked by the same function, so the fix holds for all fixed-denomination products. Flagged: variable-amount products have a separate pre-existing gap (the validator's `calculatePricing($variant, $qty)` doesn't receive the customer's typed amount — cart pricing for variable items is still the known `CartManager` stub). **Scrollbars:** reworked the `app.css` policy per request — only the page (document) shows a scrollbar (thin, modern, rounded); every in-page scroll container (dropdown panels, scroll areas, modals) shows no scrollbar at all (`* { scrollbar-width: none } *::-webkit-scrollbar { display: none }`, re-enabled only on `html`/`body`). **Run:** `vendor/bin/pint --dirty --format agent` + `npm run build`. |

New `php artisan catalog:backfill-variant-subcategories` command (`app/Console/Commands/BackfillVariantSubcategoriesCommand.php`) populates pre-existing rows from each variant's raw `metadata.subTypes` (eSIM variants, whose metadata has no top-level subTypes, fall back to the product's subcategory). **Run:** `php artisan migrate` + `php artisan catalog:backfill-variant-subcategories` + `vendor/bin/pint --dirty --format agent`. **Operator logos:** confirmed the Zendit `/topups/offers` payload has no logo field at all (only cost/send/price/brand/country/subTypes), same as gift-card vouchers. Confirmed empirically: Zendit has NO operator logos — `topups:sync-logos` resolved only 2 of 191 operator brand keys via `/brands` (the 2 just collide with gift-card brands). Operators carry no logo in the offer payload or `/brands`. Resolution: styled name tiles instead of logos. | 2026-05-18 | Roddy Tech | Fixed the admin/dashboard header floating mid-page. Root cause: Flux's `sticky` prop on `<flux:header>` does not use CSS sticky — it injects `x-bind:style="{ position:'sticky', top: $el.offsetTop+'px' }"` (`vendor/livewire/flux/stubs/.../flux/header.blade.php:17`), freezing the CSS `top` to a DOM `offsetTop` read ONCE at Alpine init and never re-evaluated. The admin shell is a `<body>` CSS grid (`grid-template-areas: "sidebar header header" / "sidebar main aside"`) so the header's true `offsetTop` is 0 — but if Alpine reads it before the grid settles (a `wire:navigate` morph, slow paint), the header is momentarily in block flow BELOW the ~478px-tall sidebar, so `top: 478px` gets baked in and the bar sticks mid-page across the content. Same root cause as the earlier "header floated mid-page" (collapsible sidebar) and "wire:navigate corrupted sidebar placement" incidents. Fix: dropped the `sticky` prop from `<flux:header>` in `components/layouts/admin.blade.php` and `components/layouts/dashboard.blade.php` — both already carry `class="sticky top-0 z-40"` (pure CSS sticky, no JS, no race); `[grid-area:header]` is unconditional in Flux so the header stays in grid row 1. Blade-only change — takes effect next request (`php artisan view:clear` if views are cached). Standing rule for this codebase: never use Flux's `sticky` prop on `<flux:header>`/`<flux:sidebar>` here — use the `sticky top-0` Tailwind classes instead. |

| 2026-05-18 | Roddy Tech | Gift-card category accuracy ("Xbox under Dating" fix). Root cause: `ZenditNormalizer` sets each product/variant subcategory from that offer's `subTypes[0]` straight off Zendit, which is noisy — a single stray offer's subType, combined with the storefront filtering a subcategory by `whereHas('variants')` (any variant counts), drags a whole brand into a wrong subcategory. A gift-card brand belongs in ONE category (unlike top-ups, where variant-level subcategories are intentional). Fix (user chose "dominant category"): new `php artisan catalog:recategorize-giftcards` (`app/Console/Commands/RecategorizeGiftCardsCommand.php`) — for each brand in the `gift-cards` category it majority-votes the most common subcategory across all the brand's variants and pins every product + variant of that brand to it. Scoped to gift-cards only; run after each catalog sync. **Run:** `php artisan catalog:recategorize-giftcards` (after `catalog:backfill-variant-subcategories` if variant subcategories are sparse). Also fixed the product-page "Popular" denomination badge in dark mode — the `bg-white/60` glass + `text-blue-700` washed out; added `dark:bg-blue-950 dark:ring-blue-400/50` so it reads as a dark-blue glowing badge. |

| 2026-05-20 | Roddy Tech | Total Revenue KPI ↔ Revenue Overview chart semantic mismatch fix. User flagged "Total Revenue $2.13" while the chart said "No revenue data in the last 30 days yet". Root cause: two definitions of revenue. KPI was `PaymentAttempt::where('payment_status', Paid)->sum('amount')` — money received, regardless of fulfillment. Chart was `Order::where('order_status', Completed)->...` — only orders whose fulfillment had finished. The user's order was paid + processing (gift card code not yet delivered), so KPI counted it, chart didn't. Per the user's call, **revenue = completed + fulfilled orders**, so the KPI was wrong. **Fix in `app/Domain/Admin/Queries/DashboardMetricsQuery.php`:** `getOverviewMetrics` switched from `(clone $paymentQuery)->where('payment_status', Paid)->sum('amount')` to `(clone $ordersQuery)->where('order_status', OrderStatus::Completed)->sum('total_amount')`. The chart query (`getRevenueChartData`) already had the right semantic — left intact. Also updated the KPI card subtitle "Completed payments only" → "Completed orders only" in `resources/views/admin/dashboard.blade.php`. **Effect:** the user's paid-but-processing order will read $0 revenue on both KPI and chart until fulfillment lands it in `Completed`; both surfaces stay in sync going forward. **Run:** `npm run build`. |

| 2026-05-20 | Roddy Tech | Admin New Users chart bars rendering invisible (user reported "new account didn't show on chart"). Bars in `resources/views/admin/dashboard.blade.php` were rendering at 0 px tall — the outer container used `flex h-56 items-end`, which kept the column children at content height (no stretch). Inside, the bar wrapper had `style="height: 100%"` — 100% of an unsized parent resolves to `auto`, so the absolute-positioned bar inside (`height: N%`) had no parent to scale against and collapsed to 0. The empty chart in the screenshot reflected that: the foreach was running (month labels Dec–May showed), the query was returning correct counts, only the CSS sizing was broken. **Fix:** drop `items-end` so columns stretch the full 224 px (h-56), make the bar wrapper `flex-1` so it takes all space above the month label, and the bar inside scales against a real pixel height. Also moved the `gap-2` between bar and label to a `mt-2` on the span. Backend (`DashboardMetricsQuery::getNewUsersTimeseries`) untouched — it was already correct. Empty state still produces a 2 px sliver via `max(2, $heightPct)`. **Run:** `npm run build` (Tailwind picks up the new `flex-1` + `mt-2` already exist, no new arbitrary classes). |

| 2026-05-20 | Roddy Tech | Fix registration verification email never sending (CTO-authorized, user reported "didn't receive the email verification link"). Three pieces of the verification scaffolding were already shipped — `routes/web.php:271` puts the dashboard behind `auth + verified` middleware, `app/Http/Controllers/Auth/VerifyEmailController.php` handles the link click, and `livewire/auth/verify-email.blade.php` is the "Check your inbox" page — but **`app/Models/User.php:34` had `implements MustVerifyEmail` commented out**: `class User extends Authenticatable // implements MustVerifyEmail`. Without that interface, Laravel's `SendEmailVerificationNotification` listener for the `Registered` event no-ops (instanceof check fails) AND the `verified` middleware passes through, so an unverified user could hit `/dashboard` and no notification ever fired. Uncommented the interface + added `use Illuminate\Contracts\Auth\MustVerifyEmail;`. Google-OAuth path needed no change — `GoogleAuthService::createUser` already sets `email_verified_at => now()` so OAuth users pass `hasVerifiedEmail()` immediately and skip the verify step. **Two `.env` blockers still in the customer's local environment (not committed):** `MAIL_MAILER=log` (default from `.env.example` — sends to `storage/logs/laravel.log`, not the customer's inbox) and `QUEUE_CONNECTION=database` (the verification notification is queued by default — sits in `jobs` table without a worker). Fastest dev path: set `QUEUE_CONNECTION=sync` (no worker needed) + keep `MAIL_MAILER=log` and read the URL out of `storage/logs/laravel.log`. Real path: set `MAIL_MAILER=resend` + `RESEND_API_KEY=…` (mail.php already wires a `resend` mailer) and run `php artisan queue:work`. **Run:** `php artisan config:clear` (after `.env` edits). No frontend rebuild needed. |
| 2026-05-20 | Roddy Tech | Order total currency-label bug fix. The orders page (and Recent Orders mobile + desktop on the dashboard overview, plus the order-confirmation page) rendered `total_amount` next to `display_currency` — e.g. an order paid $2.13 USD by a customer with NGN locale showed up as "2.13 NGN", which is wrong (the value is in USD; the label was lying). Root cause: `CheckoutService::placeOrder` stores `settlement_currency = 'USD'` + `total_amount = $validatedTotals['total']` (a USD value), and writes `display_currency` to the locale the customer picked — but the FX conversion is a stub (`PaymentAttempt::exchange_rate_snapshot = 1.0000` with a comment "Display is same as settlement or map FX if needed"). So no conversion has happened; the stored value is always settlement (USD). **Fix (view-layer, 4 sites):** render against `settlement_currency` (falling back to `'USD'`) instead of `display_currency` so the number and the symbol agree — `livewire/dashboard/orders.blade.php` Total amount row, `shop/order.blade.php` `$sym` helper (drives the per-line + total `$money` formatter), `livewire/dashboard/overview.blade.php` mobile + desktop Recent Orders cards. **Backend gap flagged to CTO:** to actually render in the customer's display currency, the checkout needs to (1) resolve a real `CurrencyRate` at order time, (2) snapshot it on PaymentAttempt + Order, (3) the view multiplies `total_amount × exchange_rate_snapshot` when `display_currency !== settlement_currency`. Not done in this session because it touches the checkout pipeline and money math. **Run:** `npm run build`. |

| 2026-09-12 | Roddy Tech | **Security incident + hotfix `prod-hotfix-20260912`** (branched off `origin/main`, pushed, NOT yet deployed or merged back). An external scanner probed the storefront from **2.28.51.145** (Chrome 141 / Windows) and raised 3 Sentry errors. **Nothing was breached and nothing was injected.** The time-based SQLi probe posted to `/api/push/track` as `channel` was bound as a parameter by `NotificationInteraction::create()`; MySQL refused it with `1406 Data too long for column 'channel'` (the column is `string(20)`), which is itself the proof the value arrived as DATA and was never parsed as SQL. A 4th Sentry error in the same window was **not** an attack: it was our own `php artisan tinker --execute` from Sep 10 run in a shell that ate the `$` in `$orders` / `$wallet`, leaving `' = DB::table(...)'`. **Real gaps found, all fixed in `5de1cc0`:** (1) `/api/push/track` accepted any string as `channel` and any value as `metadata`, now allowlists the `CommunicationChannel` enum and requires `nullable|array|max:50`, so a bad value is a 422 that never reaches the DB; the service worker only ever sends `channel: 'push'` so nothing legitimate breaks. (2) `/cart/data` + `/cart/items` echo the requested `currency` straight into their JSON, so malformed UTF-8 made `json_encode` throw **while the response was being built**, far from any validation, producing 500s. Now validated against the distinct currencies of `config/country_currency.php` (87 codes, exactly what the locale modal can produce, since picking a country picks the currency and there is no free-text currency field in the UI); anything else degrades to USD. Note production had already touched `CartWebController` (charm rounding) but left this unvalidated. (3) New global `app/Http/Middleware/RejectMalformedInput.php`, prepended in `bootstrap/app.php`: refuses malformed UTF-8 anywhere in a request and query values over 2KB with **422**. Bodies are checked for encoding but NOT length on purpose (a newsletter HTML body is legitimately long). JSON bodies cannot trigger it since `json_decode` already rejects bad UTF-8, so webhooks are unaffected (verified: Airalo signature tests pass). (4) Cart routes were the only unauthenticated write surface with no rate limit; added `cart-read` (240/min) and `cart-write` (60/min) limiters in `AppServiceProvider`, keyed user then `guest_token` cookie then IP so one shared NAT egress cannot lock out an office. `/api/push/track` already had `throttle:120,1` and was left alone. **Tests:** new `tests/Feature/Security/ScannerProbeHardeningTest.php` replays both real payloads, 12 pass; full suite on the hotfix branch **1919 passed / 6382 assertions / 0 failures**, Pint clean. **Still to do:** deploy the hotfix, merge it back into `main` + `develop`, grep the VPS nginx logs at `/home/rshoprefills-v2/logs/nginx/` for 2.28.51.145 (local dev logs had 0 hits, as expected), and block the IP at Cloudflare after preserving logs. |

| 2026-09-13 | Roddy Tech | **Translate engine rebuilt. The languages offered are now the only languages reachable, and most visitors load no translation script at all.** **The bug:** two systems both claimed French. Laravel renders French from `lang/fr` (577 lines), but the picker's French never reached it, because the modal's hidden `locale` field posted `app()->getLocale()` unchanged, so Save only set the widget cookie. The widget was hardcoded `pageLanguage: 'en'`, so it treated already-French copy as English and machine-translated it a second time. **The fix, in four parts.** (1) **One list.** `config/localization.php` gains a `machine` section (the 8 widget-rendered languages with their engine codes, note Mandarin is `zh-CN` not `zh`) and `picker_order`; new `App\Support\Languages` resolves `names()`, `engineMap()`, `serverLocaleMap()`, `detectionMap()`, `offers()`. The engine's own 88-entry map and the modal's own 10-name array are both gone, which is what the config file's header had asked for all along. A language missing from `picker_order` is appended, not dropped. (2) **The server half of a language change.** The modal's hidden field is now `:value="serverLocaleFor(draftLanguage)"`, so French posts `fr` and gets real lang files while a machine language posts the default; `LocaleController` still validates against `supported`, so only a shipped locale can land. (3) **Truthful source.** The engine emits `PAGE_LOCALE` from `app()->getLocale()` and sets `googtrans=/<PAGE_LOCALE>/<code>`, so the widget is never lied to about the source language. (4) **Speed.** The widget fetch AND its preconnect now sit behind `if (!activeCode) { return; }`, so English and French visitors make zero third-party requests; the old code fetched `translate_a/element.js` for everyone. The `livewire:navigated` handler rebuilt: it re-creates the TranslateElement instead of polling every 60ms up to 80 times (a 4.8s tail per SPA navigation) for a `<select>` the navigation had already destroyed; bounded fallback is now 10 x 100ms. A stored language no longer offered is retired on load, closing the trap where a Hindi browser got a Hindi page the ten-row picker could not un-pick. **Tests:** new `tests/Feature/Localization/TranslateEngineTest.php`, 20 tests; `UtilityLinksLocaleTest` updated for the bound hidden field. Locale suites 84 passed / 454 assertions, Pint clean. **Not done:** Google Translate wraps text in `<font>` tags, which can fight Livewire's DOM morphing and will happily rewrite prices and reference codes; marking money and order identifiers `translate="no"` is the obvious follow-up and was left out of scope. |
| 2026-09-13 | Roddy Tech | **The Google Translate widget is gone. All 10 languages now render server-side from real lang files, so a language change is a page render rather than a repaint.** Roddy's report was "the languages don't even translate, and Google Translate loads visibly". **What was built:** `lang/es`, `lang/pt`, `lang/ar`, `lang/sw`, `lang/ha`, `lang/de`, `lang/zh`, `lang/it`, each a full translation of all 14 files in `lang/en` (**404 keys apiece**, 140 locale files total). Written by eight parallel translator agents, one per language, then **independently verified** by a checker that compares every leaf key, every distinct `:placeholder` token and every plural-pipe count against English. That verification caught four real defects: a truncated `lang/ha/validation.php`; an unescaped apostrophe in the Hausa word for terms (`ka'idoji`, fixed by switching that value to double quotes); a letter dropped by a hex escape in the first attempt at that fix; and **112 empty validation `attributes` entries** across all eight (English's is empty, so the agents copied it, which would have put raw English field names inside translated sentences) - these were written by hand. The checker also flagged `lang/fr`, where **French was right and the checker was wrong**: French legitimately fills `attributes` that English leaves empty, and legitimately drops a repeated `:attribute` that reads better unrepeated, so the checker was corrected to fail only on MISSING keys and to compare placeholders as distinct sets. **Code:** `config/localization.php` now lists all 10 under `supported` (the `machine` tier is deleted), with `'rtl' => true` on `ar`; `App\Support\Languages` loses `engineMap`/`serverLocaleMap` and gains `localeMap`, `supports`, `isRtl`, `direction`; `resources/views/partials/translate-engine.blade.php` is **deleted** along with its three layout includes; the modal's hidden field posts the language's own locale via `localeFor()`, and `rshopSetTranslateCookie` is gone. **New: right-to-left support**, which never existed - all four layouts emit `dir="{{ Languages::direction() }}"`, so Arabic gets `dir="rtl"`. `partials/head` emits `window.rshopLanguage` before the bundle so the nav pill paints the server's language rather than a possibly-stale `localStorage` entry. **Tests:** `TranslateEngineTest` rewritten to 20+ tests asserting completeness (no missing key, no lost placeholder, no changed plural count, every locale names its validation attributes), absence of the widget, and that **every one of the 10 actually renders**; `LocalizationTest` had three tests whose premise was literally "only English and French are supported" and which used German as their example of an UNSUPPORTED locale - updated, with Japanese now playing that part. Locale + nav suites: **149 passed / 8621 assertions**. Pint clean. **Gotcha worth remembering:** `SetLocale` resolves user, then session, then cookie, then Accept-Language. Two requests in one test share a session, so the second inherits the first's locale; tests that switch languages must go through `locale.update` or call `flushSession()` between requests. This bit twice. |

| 2026-09-13 | Roddy Tech | **False alarm worth writing down: "French goes straight back to English" was Chrome, not the app.** Roddy's screenshot showed the storefront in English with the nav pill reading FR, and Chrome's own green "Translated to English / Undo" bar sitting at the top of the window. Verified against the running server: a French request returns `lang="fr"`, `window.rshopLanguage = "French"` and real copy ("Cartes cadeaux", "Recharge mobile"), and the page carries no translation invitation at all (the only translate attributes are the 168 existing `translate="no"` pins on brand names and prices). Cause: Chrome had **"Always translate French"** enabled on that profile. It had never fired before because the page used to embed Google's own translate widget, which suppresses Chrome's offer; deleting the widget removed the suppression, so a long-standing browser setting finally had a French page to act on. **Real French customers are unaffected** - their Chrome is in French, so nothing needs translating. Only an English-locale browser testing as a French visitor sees it. **Fix is browser-side:** untick "Always translate French" in Chrome. **Option not taken:** a `notranslate` hint would stop Chrome offering translation; applied site-wide it would also block a genuine Spanish visitor from translating the English page, which matters more now only two languages ship, so the narrow form (only once a visitor has explicitly picked a language) is the one to build if it ever comes up. |

| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **Currency handling on the "Refer a product" reward badge, after the user warned not to mix exchange rates with display figures.** The catalogue does not hold one currency per row: a top-up row carries face_value and retail_price in the LOCAL currency (36,347 XAF of airtime) while cost_price on the SAME row is USD. Two consequences, both now handled. (1) The margin shortlist is restricted to USD rows again, because `(face_value - cost_price) / face_value` on a XAF row compares two different monies and reads as a ~99% margin. (2) The ceiling behind "Earn up to N Rcoin" is converted explicitly through the `exchange_rates` table (active rows into USD, the same table checkout settles against) and returns NOTHING when no rate exists, so a card without a rate shows no badge rather than a wrong one. Ceilings are also scoped per brand AND per country, since a brand page serves one country: MTN in Benin tops out near $47 in fixed denominations while MTN in Nigeria reaches $73, and a brand-wide maximum would promise what the linked page cannot pay. Verified against the user's own figures: Orange CM = $44.21, MTN BJ = $46.99 on fixed denominations. The badge reads slightly higher ($53.21 MTN BJ) because both brands also carry a VARIABLE top-up whose max_amount is 30,000 XOF, which a customer really can send. Cache bumped to v7. |

| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **Every product detail page was 500ing (gift cards, top-ups and bills, not just the page a referral card linked to); fixed.** Cause was in uncommitted work on `resources/views/shop/product.blade.php` (the i18n refactor), not in the referral work: the inline parenthesised `@php(...)` on the similar-brands loop compiled to a PHP open tag with NO space after it, which PHP does not recognise as an open tag, so everything below it stopped being compiled and the layout component's own `if` was left unclosed, surfacing as "unexpected endif" at the end of the file. The same line exists in the committed version and compiles there, so something else in the new code changed how Blade handles it; the block `@php ... @endphp` form is unambiguous either way and is now used. All of the other dev's i18n work is untouched. Verified live: /gift-cards/apple, /topups/m-t-n, /esims/*, and every listing page return 200. |
| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | Two Blade traps worth remembering, both hit today. (1) Never write a literal PHP open tag inside a `{{-- --}}` comment: my first version of the fix explained the bug using one, and it re-broke the page with "Undefined variable $sLogo" because the comment opened a raw PHP context. (2) Never write Alpine's `@error` shorthand in a template; Blade claims `@error` as its own directive and the view stops compiling. Use `x-on:error`. |

Each card now carries its route NAME and params, and the view builds both sides: the card's own link (and the eSIM component's destination-url) uses the `dashboard.shop.*` mirror via `route('dashboard.'.$name)` guarded by `Route::has()`, with `wire:navigate.hover`; the link the customer COPIES stays the public `shop.*` URL, because whoever receives a referral link is not signed in. The two route sets mirror exactly bar the prefix. | 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **Fixed the gift card grid breaking when the dashboard sidebar opens.** Every catalog row sizes its columns by dividing its OWN measured width, but each one only re-measured on `@resize.window`. Folding the sidebar changes the pane's width without any window resize, so the columns kept the width they were built at and the last card was clipped at the panel edge until the sidebar was folded back or the page hard-reloaded, which is exactly what the user described. | 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **Root cause of every dark-mode hover bug found and fixed in one line.** `app.css` declared `@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *))`. `:where()` contributes ZERO specificity, so a `dark:hover:bg-white/5` utility tied EXACTLY with the `hover:bg-white/70` it was written to replace, and the winner was decided by whichever Tailwind emitted last, per property. That is why the sidebar hover, the filter pills, the theme switcher and the Copy button all misbehaved in dark mode while other pairs happened to work: it was a coin flip, not a pattern. Changed to `&:is(.dark, .dark *)`, which adds one class of specificity, exactly what Tailwind's own class-based dark mode (`.dark &`) has always carried, so a dark: utility now reliably beats its light counterpart everywhere. Verified in the built CSS that the old form compiled to `.dark\:block:where(.dark,.dark *)`. The `html.dark` rules with !important further down the file are unaffected and still win. **BLAST RADIUS: site-wide.** Every dark: utility gains specificity, so anywhere a plain utility was previously (accidentally) beating a dark: one, the dark value will now show in dark mode. That is the intent of writing dark: at all, but it wants a visual pass over the storefront and admin as well as the dashboard. The hand-written `.nav-item:hover` and `.refer-pill:hover` rules added earlier today are now belt-and-braces rather than load-bearing. |
| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | Profile settings audited after the dark-variant fix. 13 elements carry `dark:hover:` pairs that were coin flips before today and are now deterministic, so most of that page's dark hovers should come good on their own. | 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **REVERTED the `:is()` dark-variant change from earlier today. It broke hovers across dark mode.** Raising `dark:` to one class of specificity does fix a dark hover losing to its light twin, but it also lifts every PLAIN `dark:bg-X` to the same weight as a `hover:bg-Y` on the same element. On any element with a dark background and only a light hover (which is most of them), the dark background then wins and the hover never paints, so dark mode reads as refusing hovers entirely. `@custom-variant dark` is back to `&:where(.dark, .dark *)`, Tailwind's default, verified as what the dev server now serves. The lesson is written into the comment above that line: a dark hover that loses gets fixed WHERE IT LIVES with one rule per theme (`.nav-item:hover`, the refer-pill rules), because two rules cannot tie, whereas a global specificity bump trades one class of bug for a wider one. |

| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | The eSIM switcher's sliding thumb never moved, and the cause is worth remembering: the selector was written `querySelector('[aria-selected=\'true\']')` inside an `x-data="..."` attribute, and the escaped inner quotes COLLAPSE when the attribute is rendered, producing `'[aria-selected='true']'`, a JavaScript syntax error. move() therefore never ran and the thumb stayed at its initial zero width, so the control looked like plain text tabs. RULE: never nest quotes inside an Alpine attribute in Blade; restructure so you do not need them. |

| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **The purchase-check modal never opened, and TWO separate faults had to be fixed.** (1) SCOPE: `<x-esim.destination-tip>` was rendered AFTER the `</div>` that closes the page's `x-data`, so it sat outside the Alpine scope that owns `pick`. `pick.url` was undefined there and `x-show="pick.url"` could never become true. The page was already leaving before `@click.prevent` ever ran. Cards that raise the modal now omit `wire:navigate` and keep their real href; the one card that goes straight through (the worldwide plan) keeps it. Verified in the rendered page: 213 cards carry the handler, 1 carries wire:navigate, none carry both. RULE: `wire:navigate` and an Alpine `@click` handler on the same anchor are mutually exclusive, and navigate always wins. |

| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **CORRECTION to the close-button standard set earlier today, and the reason is a trap worth remembering.** The first version used `bg-zinc-200` / `hover:bg-zinc-300`, and in DARK MODE the chip was invisible: app.css remaps `.dark .bg-zinc-200` to the ash card surface (line 1569) and `.dark .hover\:bg-zinc-300:hover` to #243752 (line 1869). Those selectors are specificity (0,2,0) and (0,2,1); a `dark:` utility is (0,1,0) and (0,1,1) because the dark variant's `:where()` adds nothing. The app.css rules therefore WIN, so the button rendered the same colour as the panel behind it and hovered navy. Fixed by moving the light colours to arbitrary values app.css does not know, `bg-[#dde3ee]` and `hover:bg-[#cdd5e4]`, which leaves the `dark:` utilities in charge (equal specificity, variant emitted last, so it wins). `dark:bg-white/10` and `dark:hover:bg-white/20` were already safe: app.css remaps `bg-white/40` and upward, not /10 or /20. RULE: before relying on a zinc/white utility inside a dark-aware component, grep app.css for `.dark .<that class>` - a remap there outranks every `dark:` utility you write. |

| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | BLADE GOTCHA that cost a debugging round: converting `@class([...])` to `{{ $attributes->class([...]) }}` and forgetting the closing `}}` does NOT error. Blade finds no matching close, leaves the whole thing as literal text, and the raw `{{ $attributes->class([ ... ])` is printed into the HTML as visible markup while the element silently loses every class. It renders 200 and looks merely "unstyled", so check for a literal `{{` in the output when a component suddenly loses its styling. |

| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | TWO BLADE TRAPS HIT IN ONE EDIT, both silent, both took the page to 500: (1) `@if ... @endif` CANNOT live inside a PHP array literal in `@foreach ([...])` - it compiles to an if statement dropped into the middle of the array. Build the array in a `@php` block and push the conditional entry there. (2) Writing a literal PHP open tag inside a COMMENT in that `@php` block broke compilation again, so `$headerFeatures` came out undefined. | 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **FINAL on the FAQ surfaces, superseding the two entries above it today.** The rule is positional and applies by DEPTH, not by taste: an element on #f4f6f5 takes #ffffff, an element on #ffffff takes #f4f6f5. So on the storefront the chain is page #ffffff (body.storefront is bg-white) -> panel #f4f6f5 -> question cards #ffffff. Live and verified on all three callers. I changed this three times today by reading screenshots as fault reports instead of applying the rule to the chain; the rule alone answers it, and the page's own body class is the first link. Where a component can render under BOTH bodies (body.storefront is bg-white, body.dashboard is bg-[#f4f6f5]) the chain starts one step over, which is the only case needing thought. |
| 2026-09-16 | Roddy Tech | **FAQ surfaces, resolved properly this time, and the reason I kept getting it wrong is worth more than the answer.** The panel is `full-bleed`: it spans the whole window. So it is NOT a card sitting on the page, it IS the page surface across that band, and painting it #f4f6f5 reads as the page background changing colour, which is exactly what the user described ("now the gift card page is f4f6f5"). Correct chain: page #ffffff -> full-bleed band #ffffff (same surface, not a step) -> question cards #f4f6f5. Live on all three callers. A note now sits in the component explaining which link of the chain the panel is, so the next person does not read it as a card and invert it again. LESSON: when applying the on-f4f6f5-use-ffffff rule, first ask whether the element is a CARD ON the surface or IS the surface; a full-bleed element is the latter and does not consume a step in the alternation. |
